**Document revision: 1.2.1 DRAFT · 30.09.2026 · final closure (H-01..H-12)**

**Changes in 1.2.1:** H-01 — no push of internal documentation or team branches to the public `origin` (DP-0.11); live visibility re-check 30.09.2026 (DP-0.13). H-02 — package identities and the SHA-256 of the DOCX exports are kept in the package manifest (DP-0.01, §6.1). H-04 — protection of worktrees and stashes applies to the founder's machine; proposals TSA-01/04/05/06/07/08 (NOT APPROVED) entered in DP-0.02/0.03/0.05/0.06/0.11/0.14 and §4.2. H-05 — safe test profile (BTP) and known leaks outside `dataDir` (DP-0.08); new ticket W0-PR20 (§2 W0, §3, §4.1.1); the totals and calendar §4.1–§4.3 are not recalculated. H-06 — integration jobs INT-01..INT-04 and typed backlog fields (§2 W0, §3, §4.1, §4.2). H-07 — strict/CONDITIONAL/NOT_RUN outcomes per the FRD-05.9 table (§1 G1/G2, W0-PR1/PR2/PR8, W3, DQ-09, TM-01). H-08 — the test/build gate reads the server-observed validator invocation (W0-PR3). H-09 — non-circular release path F3a → K → F3b → public GO → promotion of the same artifact (DP-0.12, §1 G3, §2 W8, §3, §4.3, §5, new §5.1 RP-01..RP-12, DQ-01); ADR-10-T13 and the choice of W8-PR7 (b) / a written substitute norm are executed before F3-SRC, gate `REL-T13` (RP-04 (k), RP-09, RP-11, §3, §5.1 step 1; final review). H-10 — ODB-02: recommendation option A, status AWAITING THE FOUNDER'S DECISION, options table (§1 G3, W3e-PR9, §4.3, §6.1, TM-21). H-11 — KVARK/Waggle portfolio boundary, gate `KVARK-IF`, B2 model profile; H200 FP8 measurements do not replace the desktop Q4 qualification (§1 G3 (f), W6, WB, B1–B3, §3, DQ-05, TM-15). H-12 — reference dates and T0 (§4.3), first-month frame from T0 (new §4.4). H-03 — LOW findings of the `finish/f1` round corrected in this document: `finish/traceability/f1/05` (TM-05, TM-07, TM-17, TM-20, TM-22), `finish/traceability/f1/06` (W0 "Exit tests", AT-27 config part), `finish/estimates/f1/03` (ADR-05 review in W2-PR1), `finish/estimates/f1/04` (§4.3 "Breaks"), `finish/estimates/f1/05` (W1-PR1, T7), `finish/facts/f1/06` (W1-PR4 `:110-128`). This revision is not an implementation approval; the PROPOSAL/ODB/DQ/RAT statuses are not changed by it.

Proposed location in the repo: `docs/plans/WAGGLE-DELIVERY-PLAN-v1.2.md` (Appendix A → `docs/plans/SAFE-IMPLEMENTATION-CHECKLIST.md`). This document is a planning artifact; nothing in it has been implemented, published, billed or approved. The working baseline is G1 → G2 → G3 (brief §5.1) — **PROPOSAL**: the planner direction from the brief adopted as the working baseline per the task, ratification: founder; the option "ship current main as Solo 1.0" is not the baseline of this plan (brief §5.1). The structure of the milestones and their exit criteria in §1 are not founder-approved (brief §20.4).

**How to read statuses.** Every substantive claim carries one of the labels: **DECISION** (only founder D-01..D-18 from brief §3; the planner direction G1→G2→G3 from brief §5.1 is **not** a DECISION but a PROPOSAL adopted as the working baseline), **HISTORICAL FOUNDER DECISION (memory, date) — aligned with D-xx / the brief's direction** (founder records from project memory before 27.09.2026 — "L2 assist stays TOOLLESS" 2026-06-29 → brief §11.5/C15, minds isolation 2026-06-12 → D-12; brief §2.1/§21: not D authority, valid only through the cited D or brief basis), **PROPOSAL — BRIEF DIRECTION** (directives DIR-nn and resolutions brief §17–§19 / brief §k: planner direction of the brief's author, brief §2.1 row P — never a DECISION; prohibitions from DIR-01 carry **PROPOSAL — BRIEF DIRECTION (DIR-01, authority boundary)** — the label names the source, it does not weaken the prohibition: each one applies without exception until explicit founder approval; aligned with PRD/FRD v1.2), **CONFIRMED AT REVISION** (proven by reading/reproduction on `2af0904d`, source: phase-A findings + refuter), **AUDIT FINDING — TO VERIFY** (an audit or refuter claim that has not yet been reproduced), **PARTIAL/UNWIRED** (the mechanism exists, the flow is not closed), **PROPOSAL** (plan, not approved), **DEFERRED**, **UNKNOWN**. "Module exists" is not evidence of an E2E function anywhere. Open questions never carry DECISION: they are written as "open — DQ-nn (founder)" or "open — owner's engineering decision (role)"; founder ratifications of ADR drafts and of the G1/G2/G3 structure are in §6.1 (RAT-01..RAT-09), separate from the §20.3 queue.

**Source precedence.** Phase-A findings (`F-HARN-*`, `F-EVO-*`, `F-DUR-*`, `F-HM-*`, `F-CAP-*`, `F-UXM-*`, `F-TK-*`, `F-REL-*`, `external.md`) and refuter verdicts override S1 where they differ. All 8 groups + external research are present; no group is missing.

It is mandatory for every dev sidecar, web, E2E, hook/launch/canary run, for the root suite until the W0-PR20 merge and for every run of the code of revision `2af0904d`, and it remains mandatory after the W0-PR20 merge. | DP-0.09 | **Migrations only on copies** with a backup + recovery check: **every MIG-00..MIG-09 data mutation** (`WAGGLE-MIGRATIONS-v1.2.md`), including the G1 mutations MIG-04(A) (tags on historical `verified` harness traces, W0-PR6) and MIG-05(i) (reclassification of legacy leak frames with the `metadata.recallExcluded` marker, without changing `importance` and without deletion, W0-PR18), is first run on a copy of the isolated dataDir: snapshot + `manifest.json` before apply (MIG-00.3), a dry-run report, then a rollback test of the class prescribed by the MIG row. **The G1 mutations MIG-04(A) and MIG-05(i) are Class A** (code downgrade without data restore; MIG-04 and MIG-05 "Rollback"), so in G1 only Class A is tested against the golden legacy fixture from W0-PR19 (`tests/fixtures/legacy-datadir/`, MIG §6 pt.2), and the snapshot manifest exports the existing `erased_subjects` ledger (the revocation ledger does not exist yet — GDPR-H-05); `schemaVersion`/downgrade script for them = "n/a — no schema", and undoing in G1 = only removing the marker/tag by sentinel — restore from snapshot in G1 is not a tested path (`personal.mind` also carries `pending_actions`) and is performed only with explicit founder approval. **From G2**, for every mutation that needs Class B, a test of restore from snapshot **and** of re-applying the Art.17/revocation ledger is mandatory (MIG-00.6 Class B); `revocations.json` (GDPR-H-05) is created in W1-PR15 before the first G2 apply. A byte-identical state after rollback is checked **only** if there was no erasure, revocation or external effect after the snapshot; otherwise MIG-00.6 Class B applies, with a rollback receipt that lists what was intentionally not restored (`executed` actions remain `executed`). The migration is idempotent (second run = no-op). Rollback does not revive erased data/revoked permissions (DIR-21). Critic note: the previous version required an unconditionally byte-identical rollback and listed only 4 migrations — this contradicted MIG-00.6/DIR-21. Critic note (28.09.2026): the text required, for the G1 mutations, a Class B restore with a revocation ledger that does not exist (GDPR-H-05) and a golden fixture without a PR (grep "revocations"/"golden" = 0) — G1 narrowed to Class A, and the golden fixture (W0-PR19, G1) and the ledger (W1-PR15, G2) got a work unit in §4.1.1. | PROPOSAL (brief §12.4, DIR-21, AT-27; MIG-00.3/00.6) |

## 2. Waves (S1 IDs preserved)

Format per wave: owner/role · input contract · output · PR slicing · exit tests (AT) · compatibility/migration · risk · rollback · affected receipts (installer/router/persona/auth) · hotspot merge owner. Receipt labels: **I** installer, **R** router (smart-router primary, compact-tool-context, budget, fallback), **P** persona, **A** auth canaries, **C** crash-injection (new, F-REL-03: the tool does not exist).

The waves that require them perform a **review/ratification of the existing draft** and record the outcomes of spikes/DQ decisions in the ADR, not the writing of a new one; no ADR blocks W0; the founder ratifications and the PR merges before which they must arrive are in §6.1 (RAT-01..RAT-09). Critic note: the previous version of this plan marked ADR-07..10 as "not written" and planned writing them in W1-PR11/WB-PR1/W8-PR6 — corrected per the package state (CONFIRMED AT REVISION of the package).

- **Risk:** the quick fixes change chat behavior (D3 will fire on a `run_harness`-only turn — desired per DIR-07); F-HARN-02/03 are invisible in production until F-HARN-01 and F-HARN-08 pass → order PR1 → PR8 → PR2/PR3. The F-HM-05 fleet policy redefinition is the only W0 PR with a design decision (the workspace-only saved-agent becomes supported).
- **Rollback:** every PR is individually revertible; there is no data that a revert would revive except the tags on traces (additive) and the W0-PR18 label. The W0-PR18 label is Class A for data: no frame is deleted and none has its `importance` changed, so `compact()` does not touch it. A code revert leaves the label in place, which the old code ignores, so the leak becomes visible in recall again; that is an isolation regression, not data loss, and it is stated in the receipt. Undoing the migration in G1 = only removing the label/tag per sentinel (Class A, tested over the golden fixture of W0-PR19), without removing the label from a frame whose subject has meanwhile been erased; restoring `personal.mind` from a snapshot in G1 is not a tested path (Class B test only from W1-PR15, G2; `personal.mind` also carries `pending_actions`, so a restore could return `executed` to `approved`) and is performed only with explicit founder approval (DP-0.09).
- **Affected receipts:** P (chat/persona prompt path: PR9, PR11; recall content: PR18), R (budget: PR5, PR13), I (the installer SHA changes; notices do not), A (no). C is not applicable.

### W1 — DurableRun store, server-driven phases, actions/journal, gates/proof, budgets, restart, detach (G2)

- **Owner/role:** Durable owner (store, executor, lease), Harness owner (ProofReceipt/gates), Chat owner (detach/`sinceSeq` in `chat.ts`), Boundary owner (BLOCKED_APPROVAL ↔ held actions).
- **Input contract:** W0-PR7/PR8 (runId in events, observed ledger provider) merged; ADR-02 (durable store/phase resume/action idempotency) and ADR-03 (detach/cancel vs R3-008) — drafts exist in `docs/decisions/` — reviewed in W1-PR1 and founder-ratified (RAT-02, §6.1) **before the merge of PR2** (the store that implements the spike outcome from ADR-02 O8; PR2 development in a branch may proceed in parallel with ratification); Build-vs-Borrow record A8 (spike: ADAPT Reflow `danfry1/reflow-ts` MIT v0.7.0 better-sqlite3≥9 vs a minimal BUILD over better-sqlite3 12.6.2; DBOS TS = Postgres-only; Temporal/Restate/Inngest = separate server — AUDIT FINDING — TO VERIFY, live 27.09.2026, source: external.md §3, §8). No durable engine satisfies all 5 conditions — AUDIT FINDING — TO VERIFY (live 27.09.2026, source: external.md §3, §8).
- **Output:** run store with `schemaVersion`, `DurableRun`/`PhaseAttempt`/`Checkpoint`/`ToolAction`+`ToolAttempt`/`RunEvent(seq)`/`ProofReceipt` (brief §6.3), a server-driven phase executor behind the same contract as `run_harness`, `AgentRunRegistry` as an adapter, boot resume with validation, persistent `BLOCKED_CAPABILITY`/`BLOCKED_APPROVAL`, per-run bus, detach/reattach, crash-injection e2e (dev) + receipt script (packaged, W8-PR2 in G2 before F2), MIG-09 migration ledger/runner (W1-PR13), export/erasure for the run store (MIG-08, W1-PR14) and the revocation ledger `revocations.json` with a Class B restore test (GDPR-H-05, MIG-00.6; W1-PR15).

### W3 — Knowledge Work v2: two recipe paths, conversation/work router, validators, production benchmark adapter (G2)

- **Owner/role:** Harness owner (router, recipes), Benchmark owner (adapter, manifest), Memory owner (context in phases).

- **Output:** server-side `conversation|work` classification + mode; visible/correctable classification without silent expensive work; recipe registry (versions) with two recipes: **research brief** and **document production**; composable phase library; deterministic validators (file parsing, sections, resolvable references, numbers/dates/citations, units, contradictions — not a source count quota); latency budget per task shape (measured, not a 10-min assumption); production benchmark adapter (`/api/chat` path, isolated run, manifest); ablation flags that do not disable strict verify.

### OSS — Build-vs-Borrow records, licenses/notices/SBOM, drift (G1 inventory; G3 shipping gate)

**Typed fields in the backlog (revision 1.2.1, H-06; PROPOSAL).** The graph remains the source of ordering. In [backlog.csv](../handoff/backlog.en.csv) every edge has a type: `──►` between PRs is `technical_dependencies`; the edges that the graph calls "merge prerequisite", "merge after" or "development in a branch in parallel" (W1-PR13/PR14/PR15 → W1-PR2, W1-PR7, W1-PR11, W3e-PR1, W4-PR3, W4-PR5, W4-PR6; W1-PR14 → W8-PR3) are `merge_gates`, so work in a branch may start earlier, but the merge may not; W0-PR1 → PR7 → PR8 → PR9 is `sequence_after`; `[RAT-nn]`, DQ, ODB and findings are `start_gates` or `merge_gates`; F1, F2, the B2 dev and validation runs, B3 execution, F3-SRC and F3 are events in `milestone_gate` (`start_after`/`merge_before`). The registry of gates, events and resources is [backlog-gates.csv](../handoff/backlog-gates.en.csv), the readiness rules are in [03 §0.1](../handoff/03-BACKLOG.en.md), and the check is `node docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/tools/check_backlog.mjs`. Integration jobs: INT-01 (integration branch), INT-02, INT-03, INT-04 (§4.1 "Integration/spec sync"), INT-05 (TSA-06/TSA-10) — [03 §8.1](../handoff/03-BACKLOG.en.md).

**Critical path — order CONFIRMED from dependencies (not from equally long lanes); durations PROPOSAL (AI eng-days per PR from §4.1.1 = effort + ~0.5 wd per PR = calendar latency of waiting for a review slot and CI wall-clock, which is **not** in AI eng-days — §4 methodological notes; founder review capacity UNKNOWN):** W0 (PR1/PR7/PR8/PR9 serially 3–4 wd) → W1-PR1 (3–4) → W1-PR2 (4–5) → W1-PR3 (1–2) → W1-PR4 (4–6) [W1 chain 12–17 eng + 4×0.5 review = **14–19 wd**] → W3-PR2 (2–3; depends on W1-PR4 + W2-PR1, **not** W1-PR7) → W3-PR3 (2–3) → W3-PR4 ‖ W3-PR5 (3–4; the longer of the two) → W3-PR6 (1.5–2; depends on W1-PR7, which runs in parallel with W3-PR2/PR3 and is not on the path: 2.5 eng < 4–6) → W3-PR7 (1.5–2) [W3 chain 10–14 eng + 5×0.5 review = **12.5–16.5 wd**] → B2-PR1 shim (1–1.5 eng + 0.5 = **1.5–2 wd**; B2-PR0 ruler and B2-PR3 tuning in parallel, off the path; also depends on the W6-PR6 repin — online confirmation, DQ-05) → B2 dev run wall-clock (**1–3 wd, PROPOSAL placeholder, UNKNOWN**: N_dev × 2 branches × (t_task + t_reset) ÷ C; protocol §9.1 gives 40–60 dev tasks → 80–120 runs; t_task 10–30 min is a PROPOSAL of this plan, not of the protocol (§9.1 gives only the set sizes) → ÷ C=1 ≈ 13–60 h of unattended work without t_reset; t_reset per W run — a new sidecar and `WAGGLE_DATA_DIR` per task (BP-PROD-04) and pre-seeding of `<dataDir>/models`, including the managed Ollama store 16.5–18 GB (protocol §9.3, BP-COST-08) — is measured in B2-PR1/the dev run; the ruler does not have it, so t_task from the ruler systematically underestimates the W branches; replaced by measurement from B2-PR0/B2-PR1) → B2-PR2a rework fixes after errors and timeout calibration — the purpose of the dev run per protocol §7 (0.5–1.5 eng + 0.5 = **1–2 wd**; eng-days PROPOSAL in the G2 total and review count, R4) → possible re-run (**0–2 wd, PROPOSAL placeholder, UNKNOWN**: partial repeat dev run; a full repeat run of up to 1–3 wd would raise the chain's upper bound by 1 wd, without changing the weeks) → validation run (**1–3 wd, PROPOSAL placeholder, UNKNOWN**: protocol §9.1 validation 40–60 tasks × ≤2 configurations × t_task 10–30 min (PROPOSAL) ÷ C=1 ≈ 7–60 h + t_reset; B2-EXIT-5) → B2-PR2 report (1–2 eng + 0.5 = **1.5–2.5 wd**) → F2 (**4–7 wd**: ≥ F1 3–5 because it carries an almost full I+R+P+A+C on the packaged build, the first A cycle on real accounts and the first C with the new tool W8-PR2; ≤ F3 5–8 because there is no public signature, Deep Security or notices asserts; UNKNOWN until the F1 measurement) → B3-PR1 → (G3 in parallel) → F3 (**5–8 wd** = S1 "final freeze 5-8", `docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/inputs/S1-audit-2026-09-27.md:221`) → external gates. **Total of the G2 chain from the start of W1 to the end of F2: 36.5–57 wd** (floor in §4.3; = 14–19 + 12.5–16.5 + 1.5–2 + 1–3 + 1–2 + 0–2 + 1–3 + 1.5–2.5 + 4–7). Waiting time for RAT-02 (before the W1-PR2 merge: RAT-02 ratifies the spike outcome in ADR-02 O8, which W1-PR2 implements; a merge before ratification would, if O8 were changed, revert 4–5 AI days on the critical path), RAT-03 (before W3-PR2) and indirectly RAT-04 (W2-PR1 → W3-PR2) **is not included** — UNKNOWN; every day of waiting shifts G2 and G3 1:1 (§6.1). Why W4-PR4 and W5-PR1 are not on the path although S1 §6 had them (`docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/inputs/S1-audit-2026-09-27.md:221`): W4-PR1/PR2 are independent of W1, and W4-PR3 (after RAT-07) is merged only after W1-PR14 (MIG-08 `ExecutionErasure`, ≈ 9.5–12 wd from the start of G2) and W1-PR13/PR15, so it too finishes during the W1 chain (14–19 wd); W4-PR4 (2–3 eng + 0.5) after W1-PR4, then W4-PR5/PR6/PR7 (2.5–4 eng incl. MIG-06/08 mapping + 3×0.5 review; §4.1.1) = 6.5–9 wd after W1-PR4, shorter than the W3 chain (12.5–16.5 wd); W1-PR13 (MIG-09, 1.5–2 eng + 0.5) and W1-PR15 (revocation ledger, 1–1.5 eng + 0.5) run in separate worktrees alongside W1-PR1 (3–4 eng + 0.5) and must land only by the W1-PR2 apply, while W1-PR14 (merge prerequisite for the MIG-08 sections, graph above) and W8-PR2 (1 eng + 0.5, after W1-PR4/PR5) are off the path; W5-PR1 depends on W1-PR8: W1-PR7 (2.5 + 0.5) → W1-PR8 (≤2–3 + 0.5) → W5-PR1 (2–2.5 + 0.5) → W5-PR2 (2–2.5 + 0.5) = 10.5–12.5 wd after W1-PR4 — shorter than the window to F2 (18.5–31 wd after W1-PR4 = W3 chain 12.5–16.5 + B2-PR1 1.5–2 + dev run 1–3 + B2-PR2a 1–2 + re-run 0–2 + validation 1–3 + B2-PR2 1.5–2.5; W5 does not change the measured W-full runtime, so its deadline is F2). Parallel branches that **do not shorten** the critical path but must land alongside it: W2-PR1..PR10 (LoCoMo gate — judge quota DQ-04), W1-PR5..PR9, W1-PR13/PR14/PR15, W3e-PR1..PR8, W4-PR1..PR7, W5-PR1/PR2, W6-PR1..PR4/PR6/PR7, W8-PR2, B2-PR3. **All W3e PRs that change persona resolution or the prompt path, as well as W2-PR3/PR4/PR6 (recall block bytes, token budget, external handoff), must be merged before the B2 dev run**: the validation run selects the configuration that is frozen and hashed (protocol §7, B2-EXIT-5, BP-MAN-03), and B3 measures it on the F2 SHA, so a later change to the measured W-full runtime bypasses the benchmark condition; at the latest before F2, otherwise the P receipt is also repeated (the re-spin is not accounted for anywhere in §4.3/§5). Check that it fits (PROPOSAL, from §4.1.1): W3e-PR1 depends on W0-PR9, with the merge after W1-PR14 (MIG-08 erase mapping to `ExecutionErasure`) and W1-PR13/PR15 (MIG-03 apply through the MIG-09 runner, after the revocation ledger; DP-0.09; both done earlier, ≤ 2–2.5 wd); W1-PR14 finishes ≈ W1-PR1 3.5–4.5 + W1-PR2 4.5–5.5 + W1-PR14 1.5–2 = 9.5–12 wd from the start of G2 (without waiting for RAT-02), so the W3e chain is ≈ 9.5–12 + PR2..PR8 (7–9.5 eng + 7×0.5 review) = 20–25 wd from the start of G2 — still < the start of the B2 dev run at 28–37.5 wd, the G2 weeks do not change; worst case if PR4..PR8 wait for W1-PR7 (`gate_passed`/"verified"; W1-PR7 finishes ≈ W1 chain 14–19 + 2.5 eng + 0.5 = 17–22 wd): 17–22 + (4.5–6 eng + 5×0.5) = 24–30.5 wd < the start of the B2 dev run at 28–37.5 wd from the start of G2 (W1→W3 chain 26.5–35.5 + B2-PR1 1.5–2) → no extension of the path. Check that W2 fits (PROPOSAL, from §4.1.1; 28.09.2026): W2-PR1 runs alongside W1-PR1..PR3 (RAT-04); after W1-PR4: W2-PR2 (1.5–2 + 0.5) → W2-PR3 (0.5–1 + 0.5) + LoCoMo same-judge compute 1–2 days (merge gate, DQ-04) → W2-PR4..PR10 in the worst case serially (5.5–7 eng + 7×0.5) = 13–16.5 wd < the start of the B2 dev run at 14–18.5 wd after W1-PR4 (W3 chain 12.5–16.5 + B2-PR1 1.5–2) → no extension of the path, with a slack of only 1–2 wd; the other W2 PRs are also before F2 (P receipt: recall block in the prompt). Check that W6/B2-PR3 fits: W6-PR6 (1.5–2 + 0.5) → B2-PR3 (3–5 + 0.5) = 5.5–8 wd from the DQ-05 online confirmation; it must finish before the B2 dev run (28–37.5 wd from the start of G2), so the DQ-05 confirmation is due at the latest ≈ 20 wd from the start of G2 (earliest dev run start 28 − longest chain 8), otherwise it shifts G2 1:1 (UNKNOWN). B2-PR3 also depends on B2-PR0c (tuning only on the dev split, protocol §7): DQ-04 → B2-PR0a (0.5 + 0.5) → B2-PR0b (0.5–1 + 0.5) + ruler wall-clock 0.5–1 → B2-PR0c (0.5 + 0.5) → B2-PR3 (3–5 + 0.5) = 7–10 wd before the earliest dev run start (28) → ≈ 18 wd; the LoCoMo rerun for W2-PR3 (same DQ-04 budget) starts at the earliest at ≈ 16 wd (W1 chain 14 + W2-PR2 2), so **DQ-04 is due at the latest ≈ 16 wd from the start of G2**, otherwise it shifts G2 1:1 (UNKNOWN). W+evolved is not in the B3 minimum (protocol BP-CMP-01 / §7), so W3e is not a B2 branch — but the W-full persona/prompt path is part of the measured runtime (deadline: B2 dev run), and F2 also requires it because of the P receipt. External blockers on the path to G3 (incompressible): DQ-02 license, Authenticode hosted signing (Azure identity), managed Deep Security report, Google OAuth verification/CASA (if Gmail, DQ-06), subscriber inventory (DQ-03), benchmark judge/API quota (DQ-04, including the LoCoMo same-judge rerun for the W2-PR3 gate). — All PARTIAL/UNWIRED or UNKNOWN by status; none has duration evidence in the repo (F-REL-11). ## 4. Estimate (DIR-25) — expert range, not P50

**Methodological notes (PROPOSAL — BRIEF DIRECTION §15.2–15.3, DIR-25):** The S1 table (257–341 classic / 118–163 AI-orchestrated engineering days) is the **starting input**; no flat AI discount is applied; the P50 label is replaced by "expert range" (there is no substantiated probabilistic model). Founder review capacity (S1: 4–6 h/day, ~3 PRs/day) **is not confirmed** → UNKNOWN. **Nor has the AI-orchestrated throughput been measured** (the ratio ≈0.48× of classic days is an S1 estimate, not a measurement by this team; brief §15.2: S1 assumptions are not confirmed capacity) → UNKNOWN. The calendar ranges in §4.3 explicitly depend on both assumptions; sensitivity to the classic column is a separate row in §4.3, and AI throughput is first measured in the F1 retrospective (measured AI eng-days per PR vs §4.1.1). "Classic" = one experienced engineer without AI orchestration; "AI-orchestrated" = Claude Code/Codex workflows, without multiplying by parallelism (parallelism enters only into the calendar). An AI eng-day (S1: "including founder review and CI per PR", `docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/inputs/S1-audit-2026-09-27.md:163`) measures **effort** per PR, including responding to review and fixes after CI. **Calendar latency** — waiting for a founder review slot and CI wall-clock 45–90 min + Windows lane and reruns — is not effort and is not in AI eng-days; S1 §6 tracks it separately, as incompressible ("Per-PR review and CI wall clock", `docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/inputs/S1-audit-2026-09-27.md:229`). Therefore ~0.5 wd per PR is added on the **serial** critical path (§3, §4.3), but not in the effort-bound, because there the latency overlaps with parallel work. Critic note (28.09.2026): the finding about double counting of review/CI is resolved by this distinction (effort ≠ latency). Without +0.5, the G2 lower bound would change (10 instead of 11 weeks: effort-bound 35.0 wd = exactly 7 weeks, and the floor without latency 30.5 wd; R4) and thereby G3 (a) 15–26 instead of 16–27 and (b) 17–30 instead of 18–30; the G2 upper bound is determined by the effort-bound (§4.3).

Waves off the critical path remain the S1 baseline ± deltas, but with the numeric share of each addition/subtraction (classic / AI): **W2** ±0 = compact already exists −1/−0.5, fleet leak part in W0 −1/−0.5, external handoff for all paths +2/+1; **W4** −1–3 / −0.5–1.5 = facade instead of merging −4–6/−2–3, PKCE+state persistence +1/+0.5, negative grant +1/+0.5, MIG-06/MIG-08 mapping (retention/GC, `schemaVersion`, export/erase) for the proposal store, OAuth `pendingStates` and negative grants +1/+0.5 (28.09.2026; MIG-08 rule "a store without export/erase mapping does not pass"; earlier no unit of work carried it); **W5** −9/−13.5 classic, −3.5/−5 AI (delta of the lower/upper bound; S1 23–34 / 11–16 → 14–20.5 / 7.5–11) = IA/nav/routines UI already exist −8–11/−4–5; "Routines run through DurableRun (Loop state out of Awareness)" from S1 W5 (`docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/inputs/S1-audit-2026-09-27.md:174`) is carried by W1-PR10/PR11 (W1 counts it as "+Loop state relocation") −2–3/−0.5–1 (= W1-PR10/PR11 without the MIG-08 part); "Home: What-Needs-Me shell" from S1 W5 is carried by W7-PR5 −1.5–2/−0.5–0.5 (= W7 delta (c2)); copy lint +1/+0.5; axe wizard +1/+0.5; `run-status-labels` +0.5/+0.5 (View-work drawer is in S1 W5 "Work Progress plus a View-work drawer" → ±0). Critic note (R4, 28.09.2026): the earlier −5–8 / −2–3 kept in W5 three S1 items that are carried by W1/W7 or are already in the baseline (drawer), so the same work was counted twice in the sum; **W6** +2–3 / +1–1 = repin+arch/pull/tool test +1/+0.5, tool round-trip probe +0.5–1/+0.5, ladder ≥3 profiles +1–1.5/+0.5, detection already in S1 −0.5/−0.5; **W7** −16/−13 classic, −5/−5 AI (delta of the lower/upper bound; S1 40–45 / 15–19 → 24–32 / 10–14) = (a) sync sources outside the chosen ecosystem: **two fewer ecosystems** than the three in S1 W7 (Google, Microsoft, Slack — `docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/inputs/S1-audit-2026-09-27.md:176`). The unit is the **sync source** (mail, calendar, chat), not the connector, because W7-PR3/PR7 are also accounted per sync source: `outlook-connector.ts:2,14` at `2af0904d` is a single connector "Outlook Calendar & Email" with `list_events` and `list_emails` (CONFIRMED AT REVISION). S1 W7 (Gmail, GCal, Outlook, Slack) = 4 sync sources if S1 counted Outlook as one source (UNKNOWN; as two → 5), while the plan keeps 2 (mail + calendar of the chosen ecosystem, W7-PR3 + W7-PR7). The net is **−2 sync sources** in both DQ-06 outcomes: Google → Outlook and Slack out of scope; Microsoft → Gmail, GCal and Slack out of scope, while the calendar part of the existing Outlook connector becomes the second sync source (W7-PR7; no connector is added). Per sync source (sync engine + cursor/delta + OAuth scope + fixtures) ≈ −6.5/−5.75 classic and −2.5/−2.25 AI = −13/−11.5 classic, −5/−4.5 AI. Had S1 counted Outlook as two sources, the deduction would be −3 sources (another −6.5/−5.75 and −2.5/−2.25); this is not applied, because −2 is the more conservative choice; (b) cross-source linking narrowed to mail↔calendar of the same ecosystem −5/−4.5 classic, −1/−1.5 AI; (c) +2/+3 classic, +1/+1 AI = (c1) taint field in convert-to-work and the prohibition of an unapproved external effect +0.5–1/+0.5–0.5 (convert-to-work itself is S1 W7 scope — What-Needs-Me actions and "convert-to-work needs W1", `docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/inputs/S1-audit-2026-09-27.md:176,193` — ±0) + (c2) What-Needs-Me list (shell), transfer from S1 W5 (`docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/inputs/S1-audit-2026-09-27.md:174`) to W7-PR5, +1.5–2/+0.5–0.5 — the same number is a deduction in the W5 delta, so it is counted once in the sum; ecosystem-independent parts (`WorkItem` store, classifier + labeled set, What-Needs-Me actions, convert-to-work without taint, injection/erasure, BYO OAuth client) ±0. Critic note (R4, 28.09.2026): (c) earlier counted all of convert-to-work as new scope, although it is in S1 W7; narrowed to the taint (c1) and supplemented by the transfer of the shell from S1 W5 (c2) — (c) and the W7 sum unchanged, (a)/(b) not touched. Critic note: the earlier "three fewer ecosystems" was a miscount (one of three remains → two fewer); the critique states "3 fewer connectors", which holds only gross for DQ-06 = Microsoft — the net is −2 sync sources in both cases. Critic note (28.09.2026): the earlier text "Outlook Calendar added" was incorrect (the Outlook connector already covers calendar and email), and the counting unit was not defined — corrected to sync source, W7 range unchanged; **W8** +3/+5 classic, +2/+1.5 AI (delta of the lower/upper bound; S1 8–10 / 5–7 → 11–15 / 7–8.5) = crash-injection receipt tool +2–3/+1 (W8-PR2; transfer from S1 W1 "crash-injection e2e on the packaged build", `docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/inputs/S1-audit-2026-09-27.md:169` — relative to S1 overall it is not new scope, and in this plan it is counted once because the W1 PR groups do not contain it; the only number for W8-PR2 is 2–3 classic / 1 AI and §4.2 uses it), entry points+manifest +1/+0.5, IM approve token +1–2/+0.5 (one channel — Telegram, PROPOSAL conditional on DQ-07), MIG-08 export/erase of the pairing allowlist W8-PR3 +0.5–1/+0.5 (28.09.2026), IM channels out of scope −1.5–2/−0.5–1: the S1 W8 companion is "the IM channels already built (Telegram/Discord/Slack/WhatsApp-bot)" (`docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/inputs/S1-audit-2026-09-27.md:135`), while the plan does Telegram only; the token and approve flow logic is channel-agnostic (in the item above), whereas per dropped channel (Discord, Slack, WhatsApp-bot) the adapter wiring for approve/deny, status push and forward with a fake-server test falls away ≈ 0.5–0.67 classic / 0.17–0.33 AI (PROPOSAL, R4); ### 4.3 Calendar (reference from T_ref = 27.09.2026 = Sunday, actual from T0 — §4.4; arithmetic explicit, except for the B3, B2 dev run, B2 re-run, validation run and T_evo wall-clock addends, which are PROPOSAL placeholders — UNKNOWN)

**F3 in this section (H-09, 30.09.2026):** every "F3 5–8 wd" in the tables and the calculation below means **F3a** (internal qualification over the frozen `S`, §5). The controlled step K, F3b over the signed `A_sig` and the promotion (§5.1) are not in the ranges: like the external gates (Authenticode, Deep Security, CASA), they are added at the end, duration UNKNOWN, with a hard limit of 30 days between K and promotion (§5.1 RP-08). The arithmetic below has not been changed.

**Reference dates and T0 (H-12, 30.09.2026).** All absolute dates in this section (the "Reference date" column, "Arithmetic check" and the note on dates, the latest dates of DQ-nn, the break windows) are calculated from the reference point T_ref = 27.09.2026, the package date. They are not deadlines and are not counted retroactively. The actual calendar starts from T0 (definition in §4.4.1): the actual end of a milestone = T0 + the same number of cumulative weeks from the "Expert calendar range" column, and the breaks are remapped to the windows that fall after T0, by the same ⌈⌉ rule. The time from T_ref to T0 is not working time of the plan. The numbers of weeks and wd in this section are the reference of revision 1.2 and have not been recalculated; the schedule against the actual team is confirmed by the tech lead (§4.4).

Assumptions (confirmation UNKNOWN): 5 working days/week (breaks are in a separate row); 3–4 parallel worktrees with an effective parallelism of 2.5–3.0 — S1 assumed 2.5–3.5 (`docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/inputs/S1-audit-2026-09-27.md:216`), here **narrowed** because of hotspot conflicts in `chat.ts`/`agent-loop.ts` (W1-PR4/PR8/PR9, W3-PR2, W4-PR1 touch the same files) and review capacity (UNKNOWN); narrowing from 3.5 to 3.0 changes only the **lower** bound of the effort-bound (G2: 87/3.5 = 24.9 → 87/3.0 = 29.0 wd of the AI part; with the serial tail of 6 wd 30.9 → 35.0 wd), which stays below the critical-path floor (36.5 wd), so it has no effect on the range; the upper bound (121/2.5 = 48.4 wd of the AI part) is the same in both cases (Critic note: the earlier text attributed the effect to the upper bound); CI wall-clock per PR 45–90 min (S1, UNKNOWN); serial founder review ~3 PRs/day (S1, UNKNOWN); the receipt cycle per freeze **has not been measured** (UNKNOWN until the F1 measurement) — **F1 is planned at 3–5 wd** (the same figure in the G1 table row), not below the S1 per-freeze assumption "3–4 days" (`docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/inputs/S1-audit-2026-09-27.md:217`); **F2 at 4–7 wd** (≥ F1, ≤ F3: nearly full I+R+P+A+C on the packaged build, the first A cycle on real accounts and the first C with the new W8-PR2 tool, but without public signing, Deep Security and notices asserts); **F3 at 5–8 wd** = S1 "final freeze 5-8" from its own critical path (`docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/inputs/S1-audit-2026-09-27.md:221`), because F3 is the heaviest cycle (full I+R+P+A+C on the RC + notices/SBOM asserts + signed artifact) and must not be shorter than F2. Duration of a PR on the critical path = AI eng-days (§4.1.1) + ~0.5 wd of calendar review/CI latency (§4 methodological notes; not in AI eng-days; applied also to B2-PR1/PR2 and B3-PR1/PR2). **Serial G1 → G2 (PROPOSAL, a conservative explicit assumption):** the W1 chain starts only after the end of G1 (incl. F1), even though the W1 input contract requires only W0-PR7/PR8 (≈3–4 wd from the start of G1); reason: founder review is saturated in the tail of G1 (~26 PRs + F1 adjudication), and the W1-PR2 merge waits for RAT-02 anyway. Allowing the W1-PR1 spike (no runtime change) and W1-PR13/PR15 to overlap with the tail of G1 would shorten the G2 and G3 bounds by at most ≈ 3.5–4.5 wd (W1-PR1 3–4 eng + 0.5 review), i.e. ≤ 1 week per bound — not applied in the table. Applying it is a decision of the tech lead, via a doc-only PR that changes `milestone_gate` in `handoff/backlog.csv` and this section (§4.4.4, week 4; H-06). **Ratifications (§6.1):** the waiting time for RAT-nn is not in the ranges (UNKNOWN); RAT-02 and RAT-03 are on the G2 critical path and every day of waiting shifts G2 and G3 1:1.

**Rule wd → weeks (PROPOSAL, applied consistently):** per milestone **both bounds are rounded up** to whole weeks (⌈wd ÷ 5⌉), then added cumulatively — the calendar is never more optimistic than the wd arithmetic. Critic note: the previous version rounded the upper bounds down (G2 51 wd → 10, G3 32.8 → 6), which narrowed the range — corrected.

**Rule for compute wall-clock (PROPOSAL, applied consistently; 28.09.2026):** only the **serial tail** of the critical path that does not overlap with engineering work enters the effort-bound. In G2 these are the B2 dev run, the B2 re-run, the validation run and F2 (the B2-PR2a rework fixes are eng-days in the AI total, R4); in G3, the carry-forward review and F3 (B3 execution is in the floor of the variants). Parallel compute (LoCoMo same-judge rerun, B2-PR0 ruler, GEPA real-provider fidelity, hardware ladder, multi-GB pulls) does not enter the effort-bound, and enters the floor only if it blocks the critical path. In this plan it does not block it: the LoCoMo gate W2-PR3 must be merged before the B2 dev run (window 14–18.5 wd after W1-PR4; W2-PR2 → W2-PR3 + LoCoMo = 4–6 wd, §3), and the ruler and B2-PR3 run in parallel (DQ-04 at the latest ≈ 16 wd from the start of G2). Critic note (28.09.2026): the earlier G2 effort-bound contained LoCoMo compute 1–2 (parallel), but not the ruler — inconsistent. LoCoMo was removed from the effort-bound, and rework/re-run and validation were added.

**Breaks are NOT counted in the ranges in the table** (brief §15.2 "before additional explicit breaks"); their impact is given explicitly in the row below the table. **Basis of the break calendar (PROPOSAL; Egzakta's calendar UNKNOWN — to be confirmed):** RS public holidays under the Law on State and Other Holidays in the Republic of Serbia (01–02.01, 07.01, 15–16.02, Uskrs (Orthodox Easter) from Good Friday to Easter Monday, 01–02.05, 11.11; if a holiday day 01–02.01, 15–16.02, 01–02.05 or 11.11 falls on a Sunday, the first following working day is a day off) + an assumed company closure 24–31.12. In the windows of this plan that is: **11.11.2026 = 1 wd** (Wednesday, Dan primirja (Armistice Day); falls into every G2 window); **24.12.2026–07.01.2027 = 8 wd** (24–25.12 and 28–31.12 closure; 01.01.2027 Friday and 07.01.2027 Thursday holidays; 02.01.2027 is a Saturday, so nothing shifts; 04.01.2027 is a working day); **Sretenje (Statehood Day) 15–16.02.2027 = 2 wd** (Monday–Tuesday); **Uskrs and Praznik rada (Labour Day) 30.04–04.05.2027 = 3 wd** (Orthodox Easter 02.05.2027: Good Friday 30.04 and Easter Monday 03.05; 01.05 is a Saturday and 02.05 a Sunday, so the first following working day, Tuesday 04.05, is also a day off — the same arrangement as in 2021), which falls only into the upper bound of variant (b) with breaks. The G2 upper bound and all G3 bounds fall after 24.12.2026, so the winter break applies to them. Critic note (third pass): the previous version counted 04.01.2027 (a working day) in the break and omitted 07.01 and 15–16.02.2027. Critic note (28.09.2026): the basis omitted 11.11.2026 and Praznik rada 01–02.05.2027 (with 04.05.2027); they have been added — G2 lower bound +1 wd without a change in weeks, and the Uskrs/Praznik rada segment has 3 wd, not 2.

**B3 execution (wall-clock) — PROPOSAL placeholder, UNKNOWN:** **10–20 wd** = S1 "post-launch pre-registered ablation study: 2-4 weeks of wall clock" (`docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/inputs/S1-audit-2026-09-27.md:238`), the only input from the sources; not derived from a formula. Formula by which it is replaced with a measurement: T_B3 ≈ ⌈N_sealed × K × k × (t_task + t_reset) ÷ C⌉ + t_judge, where N_sealed comes from BP-STAT-N (protocol §10.7: measured in B2, not assumed; §9.1 figures: UNKNOWN — open: DQ-04 / Q-02), K ≥ 3 branches (BP-CMP-01 minimum: B0, W-full, one ablation, Q-04), k repetitions (pass^k, Q-03), t_task = measured wall-clock time per task per branch (B2-PR0 ruler + B2 dev run), t_reset = reset per W run (new sidecar and `WAGGLE_DATA_DIR` per task, BP-PROD-04; pre-seed `<dataDir>/models` incl. the managed Ollama store 16.5–18 GB, protocol §9.3, BP-COST-08; measured in B2-PR1/the dev run — the ruler does not have it, so t_task from the ruler would underestimate the W branches), C = bench machine concurrency for the local model (UNKNOWN), t_judge = judge time with r repetitions and quota (Q-03, DQ-04). Protocol §7 states UNKNOWN for the B3 duration until Q-01/Q-02 — that remains authoritative; any deviation of the measured T_B3 from 10–20 wd shifts the G3 bounds 1:1 in variant (b) (B3 is in the serial part), and in (a) only while the B3 chain is longer than the effort-bound part (18.3–31.2 wd without W3e-PR9; protocol §7 carries the same condition, 28.09.2026). Check from the protocol §9.1 inputs (same pattern as for the B2 dev run; 28.09.2026): sealed 120–160 × K ≥ 3 × k = 1–2 × t_task 10–30 min ÷ C = 1 ≈ 60–480 h ≈ 2.5–20 days of continuous work ≈ 2–14 wd, + t_judge + t_reset (t_task 10–30 min is a PROPOSAL of this plan — protocol §9.1 gives only set sizes, not time per task; R4). This does not conflict with the 10–20 wd placeholder, but it shows that the upper bound is sensitive to k and t_task (k = 2 and t_task = 30 min give ≈ 14 wd for execution alone), and that the lower bound of the placeholder is more conservative than the formula. The placeholder remains until the B2 measurement. **B2 dev run (wall-clock) — PROPOSAL placeholder, UNKNOWN:** 1–3 wd (N_dev × 2 × (t_task + t_reset) ÷ C; §3), replaced with a measurement from B2-PR0/B2-PR1. **B2 re-run** 0–2 wd (the rework fixes are B2-PR2a, eng-days 0.5–1.5 AI + 0.5 review, R4) and **validation run** 1–3 wd (protocol §9.1: 40–60 × ≤2 configurations × t_task 10–30 min (PROPOSAL) ÷ C=1 ≈ 7–60 h + t_reset; B2-EXIT-5) — PROPOSAL placeholders, UNKNOWN, replaced with a measurement from the dev run (28.09.2026). **T_evo** (dev-set evolution of variants for W3e-PR9, protocol §9.1 "variant evolution (DIR-14)") — **PROPOSAL placeholder 1–8 wd, UNKNOWN** (R4): T_evo ≈ N_dev × (n_kand × g + 1) × (t_task + t_reset) ÷ C + t_judge, where N_dev is 40–60 (protocol §9.1 dev), n_kand 2–4 candidates per generation and g 1–2 generations (PROPOSAL inputs of W3e-PR9b; the baseline is scored once, anchor from W3e-PR3), t_task 10–30 min (PROPOSAL), C = 1: 40 × 3 × 10 min ≈ 20 h to 60 × 9 × 30 min ≈ 270 h ≈ 0.8–11 days of continuous work ≈ 1–8 wd, + t_judge (UNKNOWN); replaced with a measurement from the B2 dev run and W3e-PR9b. It enters the chain only when it shares the bench machine with B3 (variants (a')/(b)). Critic note (third pass): the previous B3 placeholder of 5–15 wd was below S1 at both bounds, while the text claimed it had been chosen "to be comparable with S1" — corrected.

**Variant "W3e-PR9 in G2" (PROPOSAL; only if the founder, together with ODB-02, requests W+evolved in B3; 28.09.2026):** PR9a..e (4–7 AI + 5×0.5 review = 6.5–9.5 wd serial) run after W3-PR3 and W3e-PR1/PR3/PR4 and finish 25.5–35.5 wd from the start of G2 (W1 chain 14–19 + W3-PR2 2.5–3.5 + W3-PR3 2.5–3.5 + PR9), before the B2 dev run (28–37.5 wd). The dev-set evolution T_evo (PROPOSAL placeholder 1–8 wd, UNKNOWN; formula above) must be finished before the B2 dev run, so the part of T_evo above the 2–2.5 wd slack is added to the G2 chain 1:1 (with the placeholder 0–6 wd). The B2 dev run gets a third branch (W+evolved), so it takes 1.5–4.5 wd instead of 1–3 (+0.5–1.5). G2: effort-bound (87 + 4)/3.0 = 30.3 to (121 + 7)/2.5 = 51.2 + tail 6.5–16.5 = 36.8–67.7 wd; floor 37–58.5 + T_evo above the slack 0–6 = 37–64.5 wd → max = 37–67.7 → ⌈7.4⌉–⌈13.54⌉ = **+8–14 weeks → cumulatively 11–19**. G3 then carries the row "without W3e-PR9", but B3 has K = 4 branches (BP-CMP-01 minimum 3 + W+evolved), so per the formula (T_B3 ∝ K) the B3 placeholder grows by ≈ 4/3: **13.5–27 wd (PROPOSAL)**. (a): B3-PR1 2–3 → B3 13.5–27 → B3-PR2 5–8 → carry-forward 1–2 → F3 5–8 = 26.5–48 wd; max(18.3–31.2, 26.5–48) → ⌈5.3⌉–⌈9.6⌉ = **+6–10 → 17–29 weeks** (27.09 + 119 = 24.01.2027 to 27.09 + 203 = 18.04.2027); (b): 10.8–18.2 + (13.5–27 + 5–8 + 1–2 + 5–8 = 24.5–45) = 35.3–63.2 wd → ⌈7.06⌉–⌈12.64⌉ = **+8–13 → 19–32 weeks** (27.09 + 133 = 07.02.2027 to 27.09 + 224 = 09.05.2027). Protocol BP-CMP-01 W+evolved in that case depends on W3e-PR9 and ODB-02. Critic note (R4, 28.09.2026): the variant scaled the B2 dev run for the third branch, but left the B3 placeholder at 10–20 wd with K = 4 — corrected (previously (a) 16–28, (b) 18–31). The comparison of options A (W3e-PR9 in G3), B (this variant) and C (ODB-02 = no) by schedule, B3 candidate and permitted claim is in §6.1 "ODB-02 — options" (revision 1.2.1, H-10).

### 4.4 First-month frame from T0 (PROPOSAL, NOT APPROVED; revision 1.2.1, H-12)

**What this is and what it is not.** The frame shows how the first four weeks from T0 map onto the existing plan: the tickets from `handoff/backlog.csv`, the dependencies and gates from §3 and `handoff/backlog-gates.csv`, and the milestones from §1. The frame is not a new estimate, not a schedule and not a promise of a deadline. The estimates of §4.1–§4.3 remain the reference of revision 1.2 and are not recalculated here. The actual schedule against the team composition, availability and review capacity is confirmed by the new responsible tech lead (ESK-01; [TEAM-START-AUTHORIZATION](../handoff/TEAM-START-AUTHORIZATION.en.md) §1) with the record from §4.4.7. Names are not entered here. No key goal (D-01..D-18, exit criteria G1/G2/G3 from §1) is deleted or shortened to make the plan fit into a month.

#### 4.4.1 T0 and counting weeks

- **T0** is the first working day after the day on which the start gates of the first jobs (INT-01 and W0-PR0 in `handoff/backlog.csv`) have been closed in writing: `PLAN-APPROVAL` (approval of this plan), `TSA-09` (approved team remote), `Q00-h` (how the v1.2 package enters `integration/waggle-next`) and `ROLE-ASSIGN` for the responsible tech lead and the roles of the week-1 lanes. If they are closed on different days, the last one applies. T0 is recorded in the founder's decision record and in the tracker when it occurs. It is not estimated in advance.
- **Week n** = working days 5(n−1)+1 to 5n from T0 (assumption of §4.3: 5 wd per week). A holiday or break from §4.3 ("Breaks") that falls in that week extends it by that many working days. A week is not compressed.
- **The dates in §4.3 are reference dates** (T_ref = 27.09.2026). The actual end of a milestone = T0 + the same number of cumulative weeks. The rule from §4.3 ("Reference dates and T0") also applies to the latest dates of DQ-nn, counted from the start of G2.

A successful handover to the team and a monthly internal goal are not a public release. Outcomes 2–4 are not announced as a result of the first month. The receipt of one outcome does not carry over to the artifact of another (§5.1 RP-02).

#### 4.4.3 Order of capability demonstrations in the integrated build

Every week the team demonstrates the integrated build from the exact SHA of the integration branch, not just finished modules. The demonstration is performed only in the safe test profile (checklist "Safe test profile (BTP)"; [01 §9.0](../handoff/01-ONBOARDING-DEV-ENV.en.md)), never on the founder's machine. The order of demonstrations is:
1. the vertical request → context → execution → artifact → evidence;
2. controlled interruption and resumption, then capability blocking and resumption;
3. activation of an evolution result, then evaluation.

UX and qualification run in parallel from week 1. The order of work on tickets is still determined by `technical_dependencies`, `sequence_after` and the gates in `handoff/backlog.csv`. For example, W0-PR9 is in the Harness sequence right after W0-PR8 and may be merged before the week in which activation is demonstrated. In the first month every capability is demonstrated in the G1 scope; its full scope remains in the milestone listed in the table.

| Capability | Demonstration in the first month (G1 scope) | What the first month does not claim (§1 G1) | Full scope |
|---|---|---|---|
| Vertical request → context → execution → artifact → evidence | The existing `/api/chat`: recall without a Workspace A → personal/Workspace B leak (W0-PR11, sentinel AT-13 G1 part); execution with `runId` in events (W0-PR7, AT-06 part); verify fail-closed and "Completed" never alongside a skipped verify (W0-PR1); self-reported evidence labeled (W0-PR8); CONDITIONAL ≠ PASS (W0-PR2); exit code and structured server-observed test execution with the negative examples `echo "npm test"`, `echo "pytest"` and a failing test whose exit is overridden (W0-PR3; AT-01, AT-02); dataDir isolation (W0-PR20) | "Durable runs work"; `ProofReceipt` as the only path to `COMPLETED`; `ContextPackage` | G2: W1-PR2..PR7, W2-PR1/PR2, W3-PR2..PR7 (AT-01 part, AT-15, AT-21, AT-22) |
| Controlled interruption and resumption | Budget stop disclose-only with `budgetStop` meta (W0-PR5, AT-03 G1 part); truthful Stop/disconnect text (W0-PR16); outcome of the `getDue` repro (W0-PR14, AT-23 part) | resumption of the same run after a restart; detach ≠ cancel; lease/fencing | G2: W1-PR1 spike, W1-PR4 (AT-03, AT-07), W1-PR5 (AT-09), W1-PR6 (AT-08), W1-PR9 (AT-10), W1-PR11 (AT-23), W8-PR2 (C receipt) |
| Capability blocking and resumption | Approvals available without a tier gate in 3 nav places (W0-PR12, AT-12 nav part, AT-18 part) | blocking and resumption of the same run for a connector or OAuth | G2: W4-PR1/PR2 (AT-17), W4-PR3/PR4 (AT-11), W4-PR5 (AT-11 part), W4-PR6/PR7 (AT-12, AT-19) |

| Qualification (in parallel) | BTP; W8-PR1 (receipt entry points and manifest); W0-PR15; OSS-PR1/PR2; F1 (I + P + R) | public signature; Deep Security; A and C receipts | G2: F2; G3: F3a → K → F3b (§5, §5.1) |

After the first month the following do not exist: durable runs, `ContextPackage`, recipe paths, `ProofReceipt`, B2, a benchmark-ready candidate, a public-ready artifact or a public release. All of them remain in G2/G3 per §1, without shortening.

#### 4.4.5 Capacity check: rough, neither an estimate nor a promise

- **Whole scope, AI column:** G1 + G2 + G3 = 135–190 AI eng-days (§4.2, cumulative). Divided by 20 working days, that gives 6.75–9.5 fully productive equivalents. With W3e-PR9 (ODB-02 option A or B): 139–197 ÷ 20 = 6.95–9.85.
- **Sensitivity, classical column** (AI throughput is UNKNOWN, N-20): 274–383.5 ÷ 20 = 13.7–19.2; with W3e-PR9 282–397.5 ÷ 20 = 14.1–19.9.
- **Caveats.**
  1. The number of agents is not an FTE measure. An AI eng-day is an effort unit from S1: ≈0.48× classical days, an estimate, not a measurement (§4).
  2. Dividing the total effort by days does not account for parallelizability. Per §3 and §4.3, the serial addends alone amount to G1 13.7–22.0 + G2 36.5–57 + G3 (a) 23–41 = 73.2–120 wd. That is more than 20 wd regardless of the number of people. It stays that way until the critical path is technically shortened (e.g. smaller PRs per TSA-06) and until that is measured.
  3. The quotient does not account for onboarding, the availability of evaluation and judge quotas, the bench machine, receipt cycles, waiting for RAT/DQ/ODB or the external gates.
  4. Serial founder review (~3 PRs/wd) is an S1 assumption, not a law of physics. If TSA-01 is confirmed and review stops being the bottleneck, the G1 floor by the same formula from §4.3 amounts to effort 3.7–6.4 + CI/rework 2–4 + F1 3–5 = 8.7–15.4 wd. That is a sensitivity, not a recalculation of §4.3.
- **Conclusion.** The whole G1–G3 scope does not fit into the first month, not even by the plan's own critical path. The goal of the first month is the internal candidate (outcome 1) and measurement. The scope is not reduced to make the deadline look shorter. A fixed deadline or price is not contracted before the tech lead's record (§4.4.7) and the F1 retrospective, or before a written justification of why they are contracted without them (founder decision).

#### 4.4.6 External and administrative prerequisites: started from week 1

From week 1 the following are only started: the request, the specification, the cost and the owner. No step changes the settings of the repo or the GitHub organization without the founder's approval (DP-0.11). The final security and release evidence still belongs to the actual candidate (F3a/F3b, §5.1), not to the preparation.

| Prerequisite | Needed for | Preparation owner | Founder decision or action |
|---|---|---|---|
| BTP for everyone who runs sidecar/web/E2E | week 1 (W0-PR20, demonstrations) | BTP owner (H-05) | — (machines and VMs are a cost: TSA §2) |
| Dedicated VM or disposable Windows account without Waggle installed (`host:vm-disposable`, N-24) | F1 (week 3–4) | Release owner | procurement and cost |
| Provider accounts and budget for the P seal and R qualification | F1 | Release owner | `ODB-01-F1` with a cap |
| Data for DQ-04 (benchmark and judge budget, incl. the LoCoMo rerun) and DQ-05 (model/hardware, online confirmation) | G2: DQ-04 at the latest ≈ 16 wd, and DQ-05 ≈ 20 wd from the start of G2 (§3) | Benchmark owner; Model/Runtime owner | DQ-04, DQ-05 |
| Hosted signing chain: Azure OIDC federated credential for the exact tag ref, `production` required reviewers, ruleset, secret scanning (§5.1 RP-04 (c)–(h)); managed Deep Security access (F-REL-11) | step K and F3a/F3b (G3) | Release owner (list and cost) | owner actions and approvals (DP-0.11) |
| Inputs for DQ-02 (license), DQ-03 (Stripe inventory), DQ-06 (and CASA if Gmail), DQ-07, DQ-08 | before the start of G3 (§4.2 "External blockers") | OSS/License, Boundary, Attention and Channels owner | DQ-02, DQ-03, DQ-06, DQ-07, DQ-08 |

#### 4.4.7 Schedule and measurement record

- **Schedule record (week 1, tech lead).** Team composition by roles and availability; the number of parallel lanes and shared hotspot files (02 §3); review schedule and capacity in PRs/wd (TSA-01); CI budget and re-run limit (TSA-08); availability of the BTP, the VM and the bench machine; which variant of week 4 is realistic; the map of every promised capability to a demonstration and a test (AT) from §4.4.3. The tech lead records it via a doc-only PR in this section (table "Confirmed schedule", added at that time). The founder takes note of it. The record changes neither the scope nor the gates.
- **Weekly record.** SHA of the integration branch; merged PRs; demonstrations and their evidence; measured AI eng-days per PR against §4.1.1; review PRs/wd; CI wall-clock and re-runs; blockers with an owner.
- **F1 retrospective (end of G1).** First measurement of N-20..N-23. Based on it, the tech lead recalculates G2 from T0 and, if needed, changes §4.3 and `milestone_gate` via a doc-only PR. The ranges of revision 1.2 remain preserved as a reference.

## 5. Freeze and receipts plan (number justified)

**What the receipts cover (CLAUDE.md §1 contract, CONFIRMED):** installer (exact-revision, bundled Node, no-Python proxy, managed runtime/model, in-process embedding, no developer Node/Docker/Python/LiteLLM/Ollama dependency; tool `certify-windows-installer.ps1`, wired into `release.yml`), router (smart-router primary, compact-tool-context, budget, fallback; tool `qualify-smart-router.ts`, **unwired**), persona (30 results / 10 personas ≥95 with adjudication; `npm run persona:seal`), auth (3 canaries Claude Code/Codex/Hermes; pwsh script, **unwired**), crash-injection (**does not exist**, F-REL-03). Each receipt is valid for the exact SHA and artifact SHA-256; bounded carry-forward requires an exhaustive diff review — no receipt from `e4bf403e`/`b07a6173`/`c4e6a515` covers `2af0904d` (355 files, 116 runtime sources, 41 on covered surfaces — F-REL-02).

**Why not "3 freezes" up front:** brief §15.4 says to derive the number from the dependencies and the cost of requalification. Every wave W0–W7 touches covered surfaces (chat/persona/memory/routing/provider/tool-context) → a receipt after every wave would mean 9+ cycles; batching per milestone gives the minimum that still proves each milestone.

| Freeze | When | SHA | Receipts | Purpose | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **F1** | end of G1 | integration branch | **I** (internal pilot certify, clean-profile, no public signature) + **P** (W0 changes persona resolution and the chat prompt path) + **R** (W0-PR5/PR13 change budget paths; the script is run through the npm entry from W8-PR1 merged before F1 — if it is not merged, ad hoc `tsx scripts/qualify-smart-router.ts`, which is recorded in the receipt manifest) | evidence that the truthfulness fixes do not break installer/persona/router; first measurable receipt cycle (planned at **no less than** the S1 "3–4 days"; the duration is **measured**) | PROPOSAL |
| **F2** | end of G2, before B3 | integration branch (the benchmark manifest pins the same SHA) | **R + P + A + C** on the packaged internal pilot build (crash-injection AT-07 on the packaged build, brief §15.4; C with the W8-PR2 tool merged in G2) + **I** internal | frozen runtime for B3 (DIR-22: production path); first A cycle on real accounts (AT-16 real part); first C receipt; planned at 4–7 wd (§4.3); precondition: the W3e PRs that change persona resolution/prompt path and W2-PR3/PR4/PR6 merged before the B2 dev run (the validation run freezes the configuration that B3 measures, §3), and at the latest before F2, otherwise a P re-spin; paid P/R/A runs subject to ODB-01 (§6.1) | PROPOSAL |
| **F3a** | end of G3 (RC, internal) | frozen source SHA `S` (event F3-SRC; 40 hex, a descendant of the current `origin/main`, so that `main` → `S` is a fast-forward) and the internal artifact `A_int` built from `S` (unsigned or with an internal pilot signature; not public trust); no tag is created | **I + R + P + A + C** full set over (`S`, `A_int`) + notices/SBOM asserts + sealed managed Deep Security over `S` (zero unresolved Critical/High) + F2→F3 benchmark carry-forward attestation (exhaustive diff review of the surfaces B3 measures + independent review; 1–2 wd in the G3 chain, §4.3) | go/no-go for the controlled step K (§5.1); **not** a public GO and **does not contain** a public signature, because the public signature is created only in the tag run (`release.yml:12-15,63-68,1373-1420`); hardest internal cycle → planned at 5–8 wd (§4.3), ≥ F2; if the carry-forward review finds an impact on the measured surfaces, the message is bound to the F2 SHA or a new measurement is required (UNKNOWN, outside the range) | PROPOSAL |
| **F3b** | after the controlled step K (§5.1) | the same `S` (= commit of the tag `vX.Y.Z`) and the signed artifact `A_sig` from the `release.yml` run `R` (SHA-256 = output `candidate_sha256`; sealed outputs `waggle-windows-sealed-<S>`) | **I** (hosted `certify-windows` in run `R` + clean-profile VM over the downloaded `A_sig`) + **R + P + A + C** again over `A_sig` + publicly trusted Authenticode signer verified on `A_sig` + sealed Deep Security bound to `S` and `A_sig` (RP-12) + egress receipt if the tool exists (TM-24) | qualification of the exact signed artifact; the only entry into public GO (DQ-01); zero Critical/High; receipts with `A_int` do not apply to `A_sig` (RP-02); duration UNKNOWN (outside the §4.3 range); finishes within 30 days of run `R` (RP-08) | PROPOSAL |
| **F4 (contingency)** | if F3a or F3b reveals Critical/High, if `main` moves after F3-SRC (RP-03) or external gates require a re-spin | new SHA `S'`; after a failed F3b also a new tag version (RP-10) | full F3a again (bounded carry-forward only with an exhaustive intervening-diff review, never for I, C, the public signature and Deep Security), then a new K and F3b | | PROPOSAL |

**Count: 3 planned freezes (F1, F2, F3 = F3a + F3b over the same `S`) + 1 contingency = 3–4.** The same as S1 numerically, but **derived**: F1 proves G1, F2 is mandatory because of B3 (the benchmark must have a frozen production runtime) and AT-07 on the packaged build, F3 because of public GO. F3 has two receipt cycles over the same source SHA because the public signature is created only in the tag run, and receipts do not carry over from the internal to the signed artifact (§5.1 RP-02). In the rest of this paragraph and in §4.3, "F3 5–8 wd" means F3a; F3b is outside the range. Duration per freeze: UNKNOWN until the F1 measurement; F1 is planned at 3–5 wd, **no less than** the S1 per-freeze "3–4 days" (`docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/inputs/S1-audit-2026-09-27.md:217`), F2 at 4–7 wd (≥ F1: almost a full set on the packaged build, first A and C cycle), and F3 at 5–8 wd, **no less than** the S1 "final freeze 5-8" (`docs/plans/v1.2-evidence/inputs/S1-audit-2026-09-27.md:221`) and not shorter than F2 (unverified). Critic note: the previous version planned F3 at 3–5 wd and cited only the S1 per-freeze assumption. At `2af0904d` (CONFIRMED AT REVISION, `git status` clean): `scripts/qualify-smart-router.ts` and `scripts/test-windows-official-auth-canaries.ps1` exist, `package.json` has only `persona:seal` (`package.json:48`), `release.yml` calls only `certify-windows-installer.ps1` (`release.yml:1884,1900`) → R and A have neither an npm nor a CI entry; that is why W8-PR1 (entry points + manifest, no logic change) was moved into G1 before F1, and A is executed for the first time only at F2 (real accounts).

**Execution location (PROPOSAL, enforced by DP-0.11):** the I receipt (`certify-windows-installer.ps1` install/uninstall/repair/managed model) in F1–F3, the C receipt (crash-injection, W8-PR2) in F2/F3 and migration tests against the packaged build are executed only on a dedicated VM or a separate (disposable) Windows user account without Waggle installed, never on the founder account. Basis (CONFIRMED AT REVISION): `installMode: "currentUser"` (`app/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json:21`); the script operates on HKCU `Uninstall\Waggle`, `Software\egzakta\Waggle` and the `Run` autostart key (`certify-windows-installer.ps1:1960-1962`), itself asks for a "disposable Windows user" (`:2271`) and requires an absent `WAGGLE_DATA_DIR` for the packaged default-profile (`:2353-2355`, MIG §6 pt.1). Every refusal by the script (`:1724` "owned by another install", `:2258` "already registered; refusing to replace it") stops work and is recorded in the receipt manifest; it is not bypassed by manually deleting the registry/profile. P/R/A receipt runs (paid provider calls, real Claude Code/Codex/Hermes accounts) also go to a dedicated VM or a disposable Windows account, never with the founder's personal auth state, and only with a specific ODB-01 approval for that run and a cap recorded in the receipt manifest (DP-0.10, §6.1).

**What never enters a receipt as evidence:** an old persona score as a knowledge-work benchmark (brief §15.4); "the runner works" as B3; internal certify as a public signature; the receipt of one artifact as evidence for another artifact (e.g. receipts of the internal `A_int` for the signed `A_sig`, or `A_sig` from one run for the artifact of another run — §5.1 RP-02).

### 5.1 Release path: F3a → K → F3b → public GO → promotion (PROPOSAL; H-09, 30.09.2026)

**Why two cycles over the same source SHA (CONFIRMED AT REVISION, `.github/workflows/release.yml` at `2af0904d`, read in full).** The public signature is created exclusively in `release.yml`, which is triggered only by a push of a `v*` tag (`:12-15`; `:7-8` "production signing is tag-only"). The workflow accepts only the repo `marolinik/waggle-os`, a tag of exactly `v<tauri version>` in the form `X.Y.Z` and a clean checkout (`:43-62`, `:200-220`, `:244-247`, `:964-984`). It rejects every tag commit that is not an ancestor of `origin/main` (`:63-68`, `:986-1001`, `:1345-1363`). The signed installer and its SHA-256 are created only in that run (`sign-windows`, `:1373-1420`). The hosted `certify-windows` then qualifies it with `-RequireAuthenticodeSignature` and `-ExpectedSourceRevision $env:GITHUB_SHA` (`:1884-1923`). The public signature is therefore a consequence of the merge into `main` and of the tag, and cannot be their input. The earlier F3 row described a cycle: signature in the F3 receipts → founder-gated merge → tag → signature. It has been replaced by the F3a and F3b rows (§5) and by this path.

**Identities recorded:** `S` is the frozen source SHA (40 hex; event F3-SRC). `A_int` is the internal artifact built from `S` on the build host: unsigned or with an internal pilot signature, without public trust. `vX.Y.Z` is the tag that points to `S`. `R` is the ID of the `release.yml` run that this tag triggers. `A_sig` is the signed NSIS installer from run `R`; its SHA-256 is the output `candidate_sha256` (`:1418`, `:1922`). `SEAL` is the SHA-256 of the file `sealed-release-receipt.json` from the artifact `waggle-windows-sealed-<S>` (`:1925-2054`).

| Step | Executes | Approves | Input | Recorded | Output / transition condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. F3-SRC — freeze | tech lead | the tech lead declares (PROPOSAL; authority per TEAM-START-AUTHORIZATION once confirmed) | all tickets `merge_before:F3-SRC` merged or DEFERRED; gates `REL-BOOT` and `REL-T13` closed (RP-09, RP-11); `git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/main S` = 0 | `S` in the receipt manifest | from this moment no merge into `main` (RP-03) |
| 2. F3a — internal qualification | Release owner, on a dedicated VM or disposable account (DP-0.11) | founder: ODB-01 for paid runs; an independent reviewer confirms completeness | `S`, `A_int` | F3a manifest: `S`, SHA-256 `A_int`, commands, environment, receipts I+R+P+A+C, notices/SBOM, ID of the Deep Security report for `S`, F2→F3 attestation | all receipts PASS; zero unresolved Critical/High; otherwise F4 |
| 3. K — controlled step: `main` + tag → hosted signing, without publication | the repo owner (the founder) or a Release owner to whom the founder has delegated it by name in the approval of K; never an agent | founder, written approval of K that names `S`, `vX.Y.Z`, the executor and the confirmed RP-04 prerequisites | F3a manifest; RP-04 | `origin/main` = `S` after the fast-forward; tag `vX.Y.Z` → `S`; `R`; SHA-256 `A_sig`; `SEAL`; ID and digest of the sealed artifact | `certify-windows` in run `R` PASS; `attest-windows` waits for the `production` approval; `publish-windows` skipped (RP-06) |
| 4. F3b — qualification of the exact signed artifact | Release owner, on a dedicated VM or disposable account | founder: ODB-01 for the F3b runs; independent reviewer | `A_sig` downloaded from run `R` without a re-run, SHA-256 checked against `candidate_sha256` and `SEAL` | F3b manifest: `S`, `vX.Y.Z`, `R`, SHA-256 `A_sig`, `SEAL`, receipts | all receipts PASS over `A_sig`; Authenticode signer = the approved subject; Deep Security bound to `S` and `A_sig` (RP-12); within 30 days of `R` (RP-08) |
| 5. Public GO (DQ-01) | — | founder | F3b manifest; external gates; DQ-01, DQ-02 | the GO record names `S`, `vX.Y.Z`, `R`, SHA-256 `A_sig`, `SEAL` | GO → step 6a; NO-GO → step 6b |
| 6a. Promotion of the same artifact | founder (owner action) | founder, with the same GO record | GO record | SHA-256 of the published asset = `A_sig` (checked by `scripts/publish-windows-release.ps1`) | variable `true` → approval of the waiting `production` deployment in run `R` → `publish-windows` publishes the sealed outputs of run `R` → variable undefined again (RP-09) |
| 6b. Rejection or failure | founder | founder | NO-GO, F3b FAIL or expired window | reason in the F3b manifest | the `production` deployment is rejected; `A_sig` remains an internal signed candidate; further RP-10 |

**RP-01 — One path.** The public signature is created only in step K, through the hosted `release.yml`. Prohibited: local signing of a release artifact, manual signing, force-tag, deleting or moving a `v*` tag, disabling checks in `release.yml`, and `Re-run all jobs`, which rebuilds the artifact (`:86-132`).

**RP-02 — Receipts do not carry over from artifact to artifact.** Every receipt names the source SHA and the SHA-256 of the artifact. The persona seal has the columns "Source revision" and "Artifact SHA-256" (`scripts/seal-persona-acceptance.ts:17,34`), and the installer receipt is created with `-ExpectedSourceRevision` (`:1884-1923`). `A_int` and `A_sig` always differ, for three reasons:
1. The Authenticode signature changes the bytes of the installer, and thus the SHA-256.
2. Run `R` rebuilds the application from source on the hosted runner (`:86-132`), and build reproducibility has not been proven (UNKNOWN).
3. The signing step repackages the installer from prepared inputs (`-Mode Package`, `:1373-1397`).

Therefore the F3a receipts do not qualify `A_sig`. I, C, the public signature and Deep Security are executed again in F3b, without carry-forward. The installer, the public signature and sealed security are revision-bound per `CLAUDE.md` §1. P, R and A are also executed again in F3b over `A_sig`. A bounded no-impact attestation for them is allowed only per `CLAUDE.md` §1: an exhaustive review of the `A_int` → `A_sig` difference, an independent review and a record in the launch recommendation.

**RP-03 — Freezing `main`.** From F3-SRC to step K nobody merges into `main`. If `main` moves anyway, `S` is no longer a fast-forward, so a new `S'` and a new F3a (F4) follow.

**RP-04 — Prerequisites of step K.** The Release owner checks them, and the founder confirms them in the approval of K. All of them are owner actions, not a PR (DP-0.11):
- (a) F3a PASS.
- (b) The version number from DQ-01 equals the tauri version at `S` and is allowed by the workflow. Today that is only the bootstrap `0.2.0` (`:268-295`); see RP-11.
- (c) The `production` environment has required reviewers (the founder). It is checked with the command `gh api repos/marolinik/waggle-os/environments/production` immediately before the tag push, and the output is entered in the approval of K.
- (d) The ruleset for `main` and `refs/tags/v*` (ADR-10-O8) allows a fast-forward push to `main` and the creation of a `v*` tag only for the named executor.
- (e) Secret scanning and push protection are enabled.
- (f) `WINDOWS_CODESIGN_APPROVED_SUBJECT` is set exactly as in `:327`.
- (g) For the bootstrap: `WINDOWS_BOOTSTRAP_RELEASE_IDENTITY` = `v<X.Y.Z>@<S>` (`:272-279`).
- (h) An Azure OIDC federated credential bound to the exact tag ref exists (UNKNOWN, F-REL-11).
- (i) `WINDOWS_PUBLIC_RELEASE_AUTHORIZED` is undefined.
- (j) The decision on repo visibility and the exposure of Actions artifacts is recorded (RP-07; H-01, DQ-02).
- (k) ADR-10-T13 has been executed and recorded before F3-SRC (gate `REL-T13`), and if it failed, the founder's choice from RP-09 is recorded before F3-SRC; the outcome and the choice are copied into the approval of K (RP-09).

State on 30.09.2026 (read-only `gh`; service state): (c) `protection_rules: []`; (d) no rulesets; (f) and (g) are not set, as only `AZURE_*` variables exist; there is no `v0.2.0` tag and no GitHub release.

**RP-05 — Exact identity in step K.** `main` is moved exclusively by a fast-forward push of the exact `S`: `git push origin <S>:refs/heads/main`. GitHub "Create merge commit", "Squash" and "Rebase and merge" are not used, because they produce a new SHA (GitHub behavior, not verified in this pass). A merge commit "with the same tree" is also not allowed: the installer, the signature and security are revision-bound, and the sidecar carries an embedded source revision (`scripts/publish-windows-release.ps1:426-457`). The tag is annotated, `vX.Y.Z` → `S`, is created once and is not moved. After the push, the checks `git rev-parse origin/main` = `S` and `git rev-parse vX.Y.Z^{commit}` = `S` are entered in the approval of K.

**RP-06 — Publication in step K is excluded.** `WINDOWS_PUBLIC_RELEASE_AUTHORIZED` is undefined, so `publish-windows` is skipped (`:2208-2213`). `attest-windows` uses the `production` environment (`:2059`) and waits for approval, so no public attestation is created before GO either. Without the required reviewers from RP-04 (c), `attest-windows` would run on the public repo immediately after `certify-windows` (`:2058`). That is why (c) is a mandatory prerequisite.

**RP-07 — Exposure before GO.** While the repo is public, the Actions artifacts of run `R` are available to signed-in GitHub users with read access, including the signed `A_sig` in `waggle-windows-sealed-<S>`. That is GitHub behavior and has not been verified in this pass. If the repo is private at the moment of the tag push, run `R` can never publish, because `:2058` and `:2212` read `github.event.repository.private` from the event. The choice is the founder's and is recorded in the approval of K (H-01, DQ-02). Visibility is not changed within this path (DP-0.13).

**RP-08 — Time window.** The signed handoff is kept for 7 days (`:1546`), and the sealed outputs for 30 days (`:2054`). `publish-windows` downloads the sealed outputs by artifact ID (`:2236-2241`). F3b, GO and promotion must therefore be completed within 30 days of run `R`, as well as within the waiting period for the `production` approval (GitHub rule, not verified). Immediately after K, the Release owner downloads `A_sig` and the sealed outputs into the evidence archive with a SHA-256 manifest. The archive copy serves F3b and the records, not publication.

**RP-09 — Promotion of the same artifact.** After GO, the founder sets `WINDOWS_PUBLIC_RELEASE_AUTHORIZED=true` and approves the waiting `production` deployment in run `R`. Then `attest-windows` is executed, and `publish-windows` publishes the sealed outputs of run `R`. The script first creates a draft, then checks the bytes against the certified SHA-256, and only then publishes (`scripts/publish-windows-release.ps1:867-905`). After publication the variable is returned to undefined. UNKNOWN (not verified in this pass): whether the job-level `if` in `publish-windows` reads the variable at execution time or when the run is started. That is why ADR-10-T13 is executed and recorded before F3-SRC, together with the REL-BOOT decision (gate `REL-T13`, a condition of F3-SRC). If T13 shows that the variable is read when the run is started, promotion from run `R` is not possible without a change, and the system remains fail-closed: nothing is published. In that case the founder chooses, before F3-SRC, between two options:
- W8-PR7 (b), recommended; merge before F3-SRC, because the tag run uses the workflow from `S` (RP-11);
- a written substitute norm: the variable is set at K, and the only gate is the `production` approval, with the mandatory check of RP-04 (c).

The order is not arbitrary: if T13 were executed only after F3-SRC and failed, choice (b) would require a new `S'`, a full F3a (F4) and a new F3-SRC. Re-running the whole run is not promotion (RP-01).

**RP-10 — Failure after step K.** The tag is not deleted and not moved. A new version goes through F4 (new `S'`, full F3a), then a new K with a new tag, then F3b. With today's `release.yml` there is a limitation: the first Windows candidate must be `v0.2.0` (bootstrap, `:268-281`), and every newer version requires a published, non-prerelease `v0.2.0` as the upgrade base (`:283-295`, `:1817-1822`). If `v0.2.0` does not pass F3b, the next candidate is not possible without W8-PR7. The bootstrap is one-time today (ADR-10-R8).

**RP-11 — Required workflow changes.** This is only a specification; the package does not apply it (ticket W8-PR7, gates REL-BOOT and REL-T13, FOUNDER-REVIEW, merge before F3-SRC).
- (a) Bootstrap identity from a protected input instead of the hardcoded `0.2.0`. `Resolve-WindowsReleaseMode` (`:232-296`) and `scripts/publish-windows-release.ps1` (`:563-575`, `:602`, `:633`, `:699`) accept the bootstrap for version `X.Y.Z` from `WINDOWS_BOOTSTRAP_RELEASE_IDENTITY` = `vX.Y.Z@<sha>`, and only while the repo has no published Windows release. All existing checks remain: repo, tag, clean checkout, `GITHUB_WORKFLOW_SHA`, ancestry, signer subject and the binding of the SHA-256 values.
- (b) Only if ADR-10-T13 fails: promotion without a rebuild. A `workflow_dispatch` input or a separate workflow with `environment: production` receives the `run_id`, the ID of the sealed artifact and the expected `SEAL`. It checks that the source run was `release.yml` on a tag pointing to the same `S` and that the SHA-256 and the signer are unchanged, then calls the existing `scripts/publish-windows-release.ps1` path.

After the merge, the change becomes part of `S`, because the tag run uses the workflow from `S` (`:210-211`).

**RP-12 — Deep Security.** Sealed managed Deep Security is executed over `S` already in F3a, so that Critical/High findings are discovered before a tag is spent. Since the tag commit = `S` (RP-05), the report applies to the tag commit without carry-forward. In F3b the launch recommendation binds the report ID to `S` and the SHA-256 of `A_sig`. If the managed workflow supports scanning an artifact, the scan is repeated over `A_sig`. Whether it supports it is UNKNOWN, because the workflow is not in the repo (F-REL-11).

**Alternative (not recommended): Option B, a pre-release signing workflow for the RC** (ADR-10-O12). It requires two changes to security-critical code: a new RC workflow and a promotion mode without a rebuild in `release.yml`. It also requires an additional Azure credential. Without the promotion mode, a `v*` tag would rebuild and re-sign the artifact, so F3b would have to be repeated (RP-02).

## 6. Decision queue (only brief §20.3; recommendation + impact; nothing is reopened)

**The authoritative text of the founder queue is this table.** PRD v1.2 §17 `O-1..O-9` carries the same numbering (O-n = DQ-0n) and was aligned with this table in the third critical pass; where the text differs, this table prevails. Ratifications of the ADR drafts and of the G structure, approvals of paid receipt runs and the approval of the W3e-PR9 scope (ODB-02, formerly "DQ-09-S") are not §20.3 items — they are tracked in §6.1. Critic note (28.09.2026): the queue carried two items outside brief §20.3 (merging the advanced switches in DQ-08 and W3e-PR9 yes/no in DQ-09); the first was returned to W5 as PROPOSAL — BRIEF DIRECTION A23 (brief §18 already gives the direction "one understandable advanced access"), the second was moved to §6.1 as ODB-02.

### 6.1 Ratifications and approvals that block work (founder; brief §20.4 "short list of actual blocking decisions")

These are **not** new product decisions and do not add a tenth item to the §20.3 queue: this is the founder confirmation of the PROPOSAL contracts that the ADR drafts themselves request (headers ADR-01..10 "Ratified by: founder — pending") and of this package's plan (§1 "ratification: founder"), plus the approval of paid receipt runs (DP-0.10) and the approval of the W3e-PR9 scope (ODB-02; brief §10.3 requires a separate estimate and a presentation of the trade-off, not a §20.3 item). "Review/ratification in the PR" in §2 means preparation and review of the draft; it does not replace founder ratification. No item has been approved. Waiting time is not in the §4.3 ranges (UNKNOWN). — PROPOSAL (order)

**ODB-02 — options, recommendation and status (revision 1.2.1, H-10).**

**Status: AWAITING THE FOUNDER'S DECISION.** No option has been approved. The recommendation is a PROPOSAL and does not replace the founder's written approval.

**Recommendation (PROPOSAL): bounded recipe evolution (DIR-14, PRD-10-13) is the recommended target scope of the first public release → ODB-02 = yes, option A.** Rationale: the layer is part of the product thesis (D-13: evolution as actual execution, evaluation, activation and rollback) and adds only 4–7 AI / 8–14 classical eng-days in G3. Option A does not extend G2 and does not widen B3 beyond the BP-CMP-01 minimum (PRD-14-07, R09: do not require every combination in the first final run). The cost of option A: the first release has no measurable contribution of the layer, only a functional claim (Benchmark BP-MSG-01, pt. 4). A measurable contribution requires a separate, later pre-registered study W+evolved − W-full on a candidate that contains the layer. That study is not in the §4.3 ranges and has no budget (DQ-04), so its timing is UNKNOWN. Choose option B only if the founder wants a measurable contribution of the layer already in the first public message.

The weeks are from §4.3, calculated the same way (without breaks, before external gates; PROPOSAL, UNKNOWN until measured). Calendar dates and the start of counting follow §4.3.

| **B — ODB-02 = yes, variant "W3e-PR9 in G2"** | W3e-PR9a..e in G2, after W3-PR3 and W3e-PR1/PR3/PR4, before the B2 dev run; T_evo finished before the B2 dev run (the part above the 0–6 wd slack enters the G2 chain) | the W3-PR3 merge; after that merge option B requires new planning of G2 (doc-only PR for §4.3 and the backlog; cost UNKNOWN) | G2 11–19 weeks; G3 (a) 17–29, (b) 19–32 | B3 on the F2 SHA **with** the layer; K = 4 (B0, W-full, one ablation, W+evolved); the B2 dev run gets a third branch (1.5–4.5 wd instead of 1–3); B3 placeholder 13.5–27 wd (PROPOSAL) | a functional claim (pt. 4) and a measurable contribution from W+evolved − W-full in B3 (pt. 2), per the Benchmark §11 ladder |
| **C — ODB-02 = no** | W3e-PR9a..e DEFERRED; recipe versions remain manual (W3-PR3); evolution only over persona/spec overrides (W3e-PR1..PR8) | the start of G3 | G2 11–18; G3 (a) 16–27, (b) 18–30 | B3 on the F2 SHA; K ≥ 3; W+evolved does not exist | no claim about recipe evolution; the user outcome and the G3 claim from PRD-10-13 are dropped, and G3 (e) for recipe and the AT-29 recipe part do not apply |

Rule for all options: the result of a configuration without the recipe layer is not evidence of its contribution (Benchmark BP-MSG-01). The W−recipe ablation measures the manual recipe/proof path from W3, not the evolution of variants.

Earlier exports and hashes (28.–29.09.2026) are in [v1.2-evidence/HANDOFF-HISTORY.md](v1.2-evidence/HANDOFF-HISTORY.en.md) §4–§5. **Handover condition:** any change to the PRD/FRD `.md` makes the DOCX stale; before handover, check the SHA-256 against manifest §3, and if there is a difference, repeat the export with the same command and update manifest §3 (brief §20.1, §20.4). — PROPOSAL (condition).

AT-22 in brief §16 is a **change-input** test ("a new document changes the conclusion in the continuation of the same Workspace") — it was replaced only where it had been used for failure UX / View work / Stop copy (W1/W5 exit, TM-06, TM-14, Disposition A22/C12, MIG-01, ADR-03-T6) by the named FRD-05.8 test without an AT ID (Disposition OD-10); the part of the finding that requests the replacement in Disposition R08 as well is rejected: R08 cites AT-22 precisely as the internal change-input test, which is the correct meaning, so AT-22 remains with W3-PR4/PR5, TM-20, PRD-05-05 and R08.

## Appendix A — SAFE-IMPLEMENTATION checklist (→ `docs/plans/SAFE-IMPLEMENTATION-CHECKLIST.md`)

The operational yes/no list (each item = yes/no; "no" stops work) has **a single source of truth**: `docs/plans/SAFE-IMPLEMENTATION-CHECKLIST.md`. This appendix intentionally does not repeat the items; the normative rules remain in §0 (DP-0.01..DP-0.16), and the checklist translates them into a check before every PR / agent / session, absolute prohibitions and mandatory PR description fields.

Critic note: the previous version kept a second copy of the list here that diverged from the checklist (the appendix was missing the items "continuous integration at least once a day" and "SQLite CHECK = table-rebuild", the checklist was missing "no marketplace install of an unverified binary from tests", "purchase", "running `release.yml`" and DP-0.16). All items have been merged into the checklist, together with a new item on the execution location of installer/packaged tests (DP-0.11, §5) — PROPOSAL, without changing any founder decision.
